# BLOCKERS.md — ช่องทาง escalate ความขัดแย้ง design/spec

> **ไฟล์นี้คือช่องทางเดียว (single channel) สำหรับความขัดแย้ง design/spec ทุกกรณี** — ตาม PLAN.md §0 กฎข้อ 4 และ §10
> เจอความขัดแย้งที่ไม่อยู่ในตารางคำตัดสิน (PLAN.md ภาคผนวก C) → เขียน blocker ที่นี่ แล้วข้ามไป task อื่น **ห้ามเดา ห้ามเลือกเอง**
> Sacred files (PLAN.md §10: OpenAPI · merged migrations · CLAUDE.md ทุกใบ · CI config · secrets · `docs/extract/*` · i18n-full.json) แก้ได้ผ่าน blocker ที่ Wei อนุมัติแล้วเท่านั้น
> ข้อความ UI ที่ไม่มี key ใน `i18n-full.json` = เข้า blocker (PLAN.md §0 กฎข้อ 2)

## วิธีใช้

1. id รูปแบบ `B-xxx` รันต่อเนื่อง · ระบุ task ที่ชน · ตั้งคำถามให้ชัด · เสนอตัวเลือกให้ Wei เลือก
2. ระหว่างรอคำตอบ → เปลี่ยน task ใน `TASKS.md` เป็น `blocked` แล้วหยิบ task อื่น (ยกเว้น blocker ประเภทแจ้งเพื่อทราบ/ยืนยันย้อนหลัง สถานะ "รอ Wei ตอบ — ไม่ block งาน")
3. Wei ตอบแล้ว → บันทึกลงคอลัมน์ "คำตอบ Wei" → นำไปใช้ → เปลี่ยนสถานะเป็น `ตอบแล้ว — นำไปใช้` และปลด task จาก `blocked`
4. ค่าสถานะ: `รอ Wei ตอบ` / `รอ Wei ตอบ — ไม่ block งาน` / `ตอบแล้ว — นำไปใช้` / `ปิด`

persisted (P0-BE-10)" · report-derived 13 datasets skipped · exit 0) · **แก้ในเขตแล้ว (commit WIP บน feature/devops):** host env leak — เดิม `DATABASE_URL: ${DATABASE_URL:-…@postgres:5432…}` / `REDIS_URL: ${REDIS_URL:-…}` ปล่อยให้ host ที่ export `DATABASE_URL=…@127.0.0.1:5433/juneflow` (loop-runner host map pg→5433) **override** เป้าหมายในเน็ตเวิร์ก → migrate-seed `ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:5433` (ในคอนเทนเนอร์ไม่มี 5433) · fix = derive URL จาก `POSTGRES_*` (แหล่งเดียวกับ service postgres) + pin `postgres:5432`/`redis:6379` ตายตัว ไม่ fall-through ไป `${DATABASE_URL}` เปล่า (password ยังมาจาก `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` = กฎ secrets คงอยู่). **บั๊กที่เหลือ (นอกเขต devops → แก้เองไม่ได้):** api container (`node dist/index.js`) crash ตอน boot: `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_NODE_MODULES_TYPE_STRIPPING` ที่ `@juneflow/db/src/client.ts`. **root cause:** `packages/db/package.json` `exports` ชี้ **ซอร์ส TypeScript ดิบ** (`./src/schema/index.ts` · `./src/client.ts`) ไม่มี build/`dist` — ใช้ได้กับ api dev (`tsx watch`) + tests แต่ api **production image** รัน `node dist/index.js` และ `pnpm --filter @juneflow/api deploy --prod /out` แพ็ก `@juneflow/db` เป็น `.ts` ดิบใต้ node_modules → Node 22 ปฏิเสธ strip types ใต้ node_modules → **api + worker crash (exit 1)**. fix อยู่ใน **เขต backend** (`packages/db` และ/หรือ `apps/api/Dockerfile` = `zonePaths.backend`) devops แตะไม่ได้ (PLAN §8). gate "compose up + api healthy" ผ่านไม่ได้จนกว่าปิดปมนี้ | (ก) **[แนะนำ]** เขต backend: ให้ `packages/db` มี build จริง (tsc → `dist`) + ชี้ | B-036 | P1-WEB-01 (port จอ Login · email field label) — **block P1-WEB-01** | จอ Login มี Field label "อีเมล" (extra-screens.jsx:36). B-035 จัด label นี้ไว้ว่า "มี key แล้ว" ผ่าน **phrases channel** (`phrases/"อีเมล"`) จึง**ไม่ได้เพิ่ม dict key `login.email`** (มีแต่ `login.password`). แต่ค้นแล้ว **ไม่มี dict key ใดให้ค่า "อีเมล"** — มีเฉพาะใน `phrases` (และ `nav_i18n`) ซึ่งเป็น channel แบบ **Thai-as-key** (ตัวข้อความไทยคือตัว key เอง เช่น `tp("อีเมล")`, `tn("ไทยเมนู")`). ปัญหา: hook `.claude/hooks/i18n-guard.sh` **บล็อกตัวอักษรไทย (U+0E00–U+0E7F) ทุกตัวใน `apps/web/src/**/*.ts(x)` (ยกเว้น test)** ด้วย exit 2 — ดังนั้น `tp()/tn()` ที่ต้องใส่ literal ไทยของ key **เขียนใน web code ไม่ได้เลย** (phrases/nav channel ใช้ในฝั่ง web ไม่ได้ภายใต้ hook นี้). สรุป: email label ไม่มี key ที่ web ใช้ได้ · web ใช้ได้เฉพาะ **dict key (ASCII)** เท่านั้น · ตาม PLAN §0 กฎ 2 + zone CLAUDE.md — string ไม่มี key ใช้ได้ = ห้ามเดา ห้าม hardcode ไทย ห้าม bypass hook → escalate. อีก 19/20 string map เข้า dict key ได้ครบ (login.* + app.name + common.lang) เหลือ label เดียวนี้ที่ block. **หมายเหตุกว้าง:** channel `tn`(nav) / `tp`(phrases) ทั้งชุดใช้ใน web UI code ไม่ได้ภายใต้ i18n-guard — flag ให้ platform/devops ทราบ | (ก) **[แนะนำ]** Wei เพิ่ม **dict key `login.email` = "อีเมล"** (th/zh/en/ar = ไทย verbatim ตามแนว B-035 · สมมาตรกับ `login.password`) ลง `i18n-full.json` — web ใช้ `t("login.email")` · unblock ทันที trivial · (ข) Wei/platform ปรับ `i18n-guard.sh` ให้อนุญาต literal ไทยเฉพาะใน `tp(...)/tn(...)` (allowlist) เพื่อให้ phrases/nav channel ใช้ใน web ได้ตามที่ B-035 วางไว้ (แก้ hook = devops/platform zone) · (ค) directive อื่นจาก Wei | (ก) P1-WEB-01 login ship แล้ว = resolved · dict key `login.email` = "อีเมล" มีอยู่แล้วทั้ง 2 สำเนา (B-036 = no-op · formalize provenance) (Wei 13 ก.ค.) | ตอบแล้ว — นำไปใช้ (login ship แล้ว · login.email มีแล้ว) |
| B-038 | P1-BE-01 (gate 4.5 rework) — **BETTER_AUTH_SECRET provisioning บน compose dev (cross-zone backend→devops)** | gate 4.5 ตัดสินว่า code-level fallback secret ที่ทำงานเงียบใน production ขัด infra/CLAUDE.md ("no secrets in repo / everything via env") → backend rework แล้ว: api **fail fast ตอน boot** เมื่อ `NODE_ENV=production` + ไม่มี `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` (dev fallback เหลือเฉพาะ non-production + boot warning · พิสูจน์แล้ว: prod boot ไม่มี secret → exit 1 พร้อม error ชัด · prod + secret → boot ปกติ) — ผลข้างเคียงที่ตั้งใจ: **compose dev (api image = NODE_ENV=production ใน Dockerfile) จะ boot ไม่ได้จนกว่า provision secret** และ `infra/docker-compose.yml` อยู่เขต devops (backend แก้ข้ามเขตไม่ได้) — ให้ provision อย่างไร | (ก) **[แนะนำ · convention เดียวกับ POSTGRES_PASSWORD dev default]** devops เพิ่ม `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET: ${BETTER_AUTH_SECRET:-juneflow-dev-secret}` ใน services `api` + `worker` ของ `infra/docker-compose.yml` (host env / env_file ของ infra override ได้) · ฝั่ง code คง fail-fast ใน production ตามที่ rework แล้ว · (ข) Wei กำหนดวิธี provisioning เอง (เช่น บังคับผ่าน env file ของ infra เท่านั้น — compose ไม่ boot จนกว่าสร้างไฟล์) · (ค) Wei อนุมัติ code-level fallback แบบ commit รอบแรก (ยอมรับ silent prod risk — reviewer ไม่แนะนำ) | — | ✅ Wei ตัดสิน (ก) 2026-07-13 — devops เพิ่ม `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET: ${BETTER_AUTH_SECRET:-juneflow-dev-secret}` ใน api+worker (convention เดียวกับ POSTGRES_PASSWORD) · code คง fail-fast ใน prod · task P0-DEV-06 (ready) · G2-live re-verify หลัง compose |

| B-167 | 🔴 P3 sales.down concurrent double-post — SEQ MODEL CAN'T DEDUP (money · design/spec · b163 live-proven · BLOCKS P3 promote) | The B-165 seq-unique fix (0043) is category-mismatched: booking dedups because a unit is booked ONCE (natural key `booking:<unitId>`), but a unit legitimately has MANY down instalments → **no natural once-key**. b163 live @ bb7d1f4 proves it: DOWN race `[201,409,201]` = simultaneous readers collide on seq=1 (1×409) but a serialized reader (count read AFTER a prior commit) gets seq=2 → escapes → 2nd instalment + 2nd receipt JV = double-post. Neither seq-unique (closes only simultaneous) NOR SELECT FOR UPDATE (serializes → seq 1/2/3 → ALL commit) yields "exactly one wins" — that requires an explicit idempotency mechanism. This is the money bug the whole booking/down/deal race work was meant to close; it is NOT closed for down. | (ก) **idempotency key**: web sends a per-submit UUID; server `unique(idempotency_key)` → concurrent identical submits collapse to one 201, rest 409 (general-correct double-submit guard; needs a contract field — likely sacred openapi + web change) · (ข) **first-down-once**: treat the FIRST down as book-once — dedup on a stable `down-initial:<unitId>` key (no seq) so concurrent first-downs collide to one; subsequent legit downs use seq under FOR UPDATE (special-cases first down) · (ค) **downs are distinct**: accept concurrent downs = distinct instalments; relax the b163 DOWN assertion to "no 5xx · N distinct seqs" + rely on web double-submit-disable (SPEC call — needs prototype confirm that duplicate down submission is NOT expected to dedup) · **spec-scout should read `pototype/*sales*`/down flow for the submission/dedup model BEFORE ruling.** ✅ **SPEC-SCOUT DONE (ade802f):** prototype `DownPaymentReceiveForm` makes the client **SELECT the instalment number** — `<Field label="งวดที่"><Select value="งวด 8 จาก 10">` (`sales-process.jsx:465`); down is a fixed **plan of งวด N/M** (`down[]`, progress done/total). Each receipt maps to a PRE-DEFINED instalment slot, not a server-appended row. No idempotency token, no double-submit guard (mock artifact — NOT a licence to double-post). First-down NOT special. → **natural key = `(sales_unit_id, instalment_no)`** (every งวด once — broader than "first-down-once"). Contract `createSalesDown` requestBody = `Entity` (opaque, B-014) → adding an `instalment_no` field is **NOT a sacred openapi change**. Correct fix = backend keys on a CLIENT-PROVIDED `instalment_no` (the selected งวด) + `unique(sales_unit_id, instalment_no)` — a STABLE key → no serialized-reader escape (root was `seq=count+1` server-derived+mutable). Needs orch-A (backend) + likely orch-C (web SalesDown sends the selected งวด). **RECOMMEND (ข-expanded).** ✅ **RESOLVED Wei=ข-ขยาย (2026-07-27):** natural key **`(sales_unit_id, instalment_no)`**, client-provided. **KEY SIMPLIFICATION: migration 0043 `unique(sales_unit_id, seq)` ALREADY EXISTS** → the fix is small: (1) orch-A backend `createSalesDown` reads a CLIENT-provided instalment number from the (opaque `Entity`) body and uses it as `seq` — DROP `seq = count+1` (the mutable server-derive that caused the serialized-escape); validate 1..plan_total; the existing 0043 unique index then closes the race with a STABLE key. (2) orch-C web `SalesDown` sends the selected `งวดที่ N` on submit. NO new migration, NO sacred openapi. Then **orch-B re-runs b163 DOWN live to confirm [201,409,…] → exactly one wins.** ⚠ **+SECOND DEFECT in the SAME handler (P3 pre-promote audit · CONFIRMED high ×2 skeptics + orch-B ground-truth):** `createSalesDown` reads `unit`/`existingDown` OUTSIDE the tx (land-sales.ts:~652/655) then inside the tx overwrites `sales_unit.down` jsonb wholesale with `[...existingDown, new]` (:~713/714). Two concurrent downs both read `existingDown=[X]` → last-writer-wins → the jsonb mirror LOSES an instalment even though both down_payment_txn rows + JVs commit. `listDowns` (GET /sales/downs) reads the JSONB mirror → **under-reports the down schedule (wrong done/total)**. The B-167 seq/instalment_no fix does NOT close this (two DISTINCT legit งวด recorded concurrently still clobber). **FIX IN THE SAME REWORK:** read `existingDown` INSIDE the tx with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the sales_unit row, OR (cleaner) derive GET /sales/downs from `down_payment_txn` (authoritative) instead of the jsonb mirror. | ✅ **RESOLVED + PROMOTED → main 38f6b7a (22nd 0-drift, 2026-07-27):** backend 6a169b7 (client instalment_no AS seq · drop count+1 · GET reads authoritative down_payment_txn) + web 648dfd5 (งวดที่ selector · sends instalment_no+seq) → dev f09b6f0. PROVEN LIVE: b163 DOWN 3/3 @ f09b6f0 (concurrent SAME instalment_no → exactly one · read-model consistent) · gate-4.5 backend+web PASS (NULL-seq positive-int validation load-bearing). Money double-post CLOSED on main. | 2026-07-27 |

| B-203 | Phase-6 W1b — Full/Enterprise plan `["*"]` menu wildcard vs the edit tree (non-block · fidelity nuance) | The Full/Enterprise package is seeded with `menus:["*"]` (packages.ts L110 · a live-all wildcard the tenant gate + `expandMenus` resolve to every nav id). The ported pkg-builder edit form seeds the tree from `preset.menus` verbatim (prototype-faithful — L116), so editing Full shows a 0-checked tree (`1/total` header) and the `*` has no checkbox to remove. Consequence: an owner who narrows Full by TICKING items saves `["*", ...ticks]` → `expandMenus` still releases ALL menus (the restriction is masked; the post-save card visibly shows all-menus, so it's not silent-corrupt but the tree UX is misleading). Inventing a 4th colour would be redesigning a screen the prototype fixes. **Ruling requested (2) — DESIGN-FIDELITY, raised in review round 2 and previously only in a chat report, which is why it is now on this row:** the installment caption `wo.list.atContractPct` ("ที่ {pct}% ของสัญญา") is rendered as the CUMULATIVE contract share (Σ pct over seq ≤ this one), not as that installment's OWN pct. Authority for cumulative: the server's own `subcon.ts progressWarning` ("cumTarget = Σ pct of periods with seq ≤ this seq") and `subcon.progressLegend` ("% สะสมของแต่ละงวด"), plus the phrasing "ของสัญญา" reading as a threshold. Authority against: the prototype's own mock (po-wo.jsx L381-386) prints p = 10/20/30/78/100 against per-period shares of 10/20/30/30/10 — rows 1-3 match the PER-PERIOD share exactly (cumulative would be 10/30/60/90/100); the mock is internally inconsistent (row 4 is `wo.gist`, row 5 is 100) and `subcon.progressLegend` belongs to a different screen. **This visibly moves numbers on the seed WO (WO-2026-0042, pct 20/30/25/25): 20/30/25/25 → 20/50/75/100** — i.e. it lands in the same G5 re-baseline being requested below, which is why it must be ruled on before that baseline is taken. **FYI, NOT fixed here (same defect shape, other screens — each needs its own slice + its own G5 baseline; a concurrent slice owns po.list):** (i) `poWire`'s LIST path emits `doc_date` and, via `sumBillings`, `paid` + `deposit` (po.ts `app.get("/po")`), which `po-list.tsx` still em-dashes (cells at L444-449 + the docDate stat at L494) and `po-wo-rows.ts` still documented as absent; (ii) `subcon-accept-rows.ts cumMap` (L248-256) accumulates `pct` with NO per-element guard at all — no basis check, no pct-recorded check, no seq-ordinal check — and its render gate `method === "percent"` is itself `periodMethod()`, which returns the FIRST period's basis as the whole plan's, so a mixed plan passes the gate and the markers silently absorb rows that carry no contract share (and a Σ > 100 positions a marker off-canvas at `left: {cum}%`, subcon-accept.tsx L434-441); (iii) `subcon-progress.tsx` L652 reads `p.seq === 0` as the down-payment row on the same unvalidated `seq` column (see B-284). **Pixels change** (scope text, progress bars, installment rows now render) → a Wei-approved G5 re-baseline of `wo.list` is required; nothing under `tests/visual/` was touched. | Ruling 1: (a) keep the 3-state collapse, rejected renders not-done (current behaviour) · (b) add a 4th danger-toned state for rejected (reuses the existing subcon statusTone("rejected") tokens, but is a prototype deviation). Ruling 2: (c) keep CUMULATIVE (current behaviour · matches the server's cumTarget and subcon.progressLegend · seed WO reads 20/50/75/100) · (d) switch to the installment's OWN pct (matches rows 1-3 of the prototype mock · seed WO reads 20/30/25/25 · one-line change to `cumulativeContractPct`) · (e) withhold the caption entirely (em-dash) until the semantic is settled — costs a prototype element but asserts nothing | — | รอ Wei — non-blocking |
| B-284 | work_period `seq` + `pct` are unvalidated and non-unique — every ordinal/cumulative render in the repo trusts them (web wo.list now withholds; subcon.accept + subcon.progress still trust) | **Root cause is the column contract, not any one screen.** `work_period.seq` is `integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` with **no `unique(contract_id, seq)`** (`packages/db/src/schema/subcon.ts` — the index list is `(contract_id, status)` only) and `work_period.pct` is `numeric(6,3) NOT NULL DEFAULT '0'`. `POST /subcon/contracts` writes both straight through with no validation at all: `seq: toNum(pick(p, "seq")) ?? 0` and `pct: String(toNum(pick(p, "pct")) ?? 0)` (`apps/api/src/routes/subcon.ts` L661/663) — no per-period `seq` required/positive check, no per-period `pct > 0` check, no Σ pct = 100 check. So a client that posts a `periods` array without `seq` persists a plan whose every row is seq 0, duplicate seqs are legal, and a percent-basis plan with an unrecorded share (pct 30 / 0 / 40) or an impossible one (Σ 250) is legal. Reproduced against branch HEAD before the fix: all-seq-0 plan → every installment captioned "ที่ 100% ของสัญญา" and every row labelled ดาวน์; pct 30/0/40 → installment 2's caption byte-identical to installment 1's; Σ 250 → "ที่ 250% ของสัญญา". **web wo.list is fixed defensively** (`cumulativeContractPct` + `hasOrdinalSeq`, per-element guards, withhold → em-dash) but that is a screen-local mitigation of a data-contract hole, and the sweep found two more readers of the same columns that still trust them: `subcon-accept-rows.ts cumMap` (no basis / pct / seq guard, gated only by `periodMethod()` = the FIRST period's basis) and `subcon-progress.tsx` L652 (`p.seq === 0` → ดาวน์). Neither is touched here — each is a different screen with its own G5 baseline, the same boundary under which po.list was left to its own slice. **money = NONE**: nothing here originates or posts an amount; `amount` is the server column and the WO `progress` is server-derived. The exposure is a wrong-but-plausible *displayed* contract share / ordinal, which is exactly what the em-dash now prevents on wo.list. | (ก) close it at the source — SACRED migration adding `unique(contract_id, seq)` on work_period + validate in `POST /subcon/contracts` (seq required, non-negative, distinct within the posted array; percent-basis periods require `pct > 0` and Σ pct ≤ 100) → every reader becomes safe and the web guards stay as defence-in-depth · (ข) validation only, no unique index (cheaper, no migration; still leaves a direct DB writer able to break it) · (ค) leave the contract as-is and port the wo.list per-element guard pattern to `cumMap` + `subcon-progress` in their own slices (web-only, moves two G5 baselines) · (ง) accept: not seed-reachable today (the seeded plans write `seq: si + 1` and the one percent contract is pct 20/30/25/25 = 100), revisit when a non-web client posts periods | — | รอ Wei — ไม่ block งาน (wo.list ships safe either way; the two other readers are pre-existing and unchanged) |

The ONLY route that writes them is `POST /pm/workorders/:id/close` (`apps/api/src/routes/pm.ts` L761-811) — there is no notes-only endpoint. **Today that call is exactly "set the close columns the body carries" and nothing more:** pm_workorder has NO status and NO certificate column and the handler's own comment says close never invents one (L754-758); its single side effect is `lineNotifyStub("pm.workorder.close", id)`, verified a NO-OP (L218-220). The screen therefore sends `{cause, fix, advice}` only (never `signature` — that column belongs to pm-close) and the server tells it nothing about a closed job, so its honest states are exactly saved / queued / failed. **Correction (review round 2):** round 1 of this row asserted that the screen "claims nothing about a certificate, a report, or a closed job" while its primary button still read **`ไปสรุป + ปิดงาน`** ('go to the summary + close the job') on a control that saves and stays put. Second consequence of `…/close` being the only writer, disclosed here rather than left silent: the body must send **all three keys always** (the handler keys off presence, so an omitted key could never CLEAR a field the technician emptied), which makes the save a whole-form overwrite — so a work order the screen could not READ withholds the form and the save entirely, and the offline write is only reachable after a successful read in that session. | B-282 | P1-BE-01 · auth production chain — invite/forgot/reset has NO delivery transport (the seam is a NO-OP) | The chain itself is merged and tested: POST /users provisions a passwordless credential, POST /auth/forgot mints a token, POST /auth/reset consumes it single-use, kills every live session and lets the user log in. **What does not exist is delivery.** `ResetDelivery` (auth-provisioning.ts:296) defaults to a NO-OP and `index.ts:78-89` logs a boot warn saying so, so /auth/forgot answers its uniform 200 and mails nothing, and an invited user (`status: invited`, password NULL) has no way to receive their link — only an admin reset (POST /admin/users/{id}/reset-password) can hand one out, and that also has no transport. B-269 landed the **message** half (`EmailNotificationAdapter` → `SmtpEnvelope` over an injected `SmtpTransport`); the **wire** half is still gated on B-269's own ruling. **HARD CONSTRAINT on whoever wires this — the wiring must ENQUEUE, not await.** /auth/forgot deliberately returns the identical 200 for a known and an unknown address (no account-existence oracle); awaiting an SMTP send inside the handler makes the known-address branch measurably slower and turns that uniform 200 into a **timing oracle that enumerates every registered address**. The send must be handed to a queue/worker and the handler must return without waiting for it. Also: a concurrent `feature/api-email-adapter` branch exists; "nothing in the workspace depends on @juneflow/notifications" is true of dev today but may not stay true — coordinate before wiring. | (ก) wire `ResetDelivery` → the B-269 adapter behind an **enqueue-and-return** hop (handler enqueues, a worker drains and sends) — recommended; the only option that keeps the uniform 200 constant-time · (ข) wire it synchronously inside the request — simplest, but it ships the enumeration oracle described above; recorded only so the trade-off is explicit · (ค) defer until B-269 picks a transport — the chain stays complete but undeliverable; invites are handed out out-of-band | (pending) | 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · non-blocking (the chain is merged, audited and unit-proven; delivery is a deliberate seam, and the boot warn says so) |
| B-283 | P1-BE-01 · POST /users invite — `auth_user.email` is UNIQUE platform-wide, not per company | Migration 0008 makes `auth_user.email` unique across the WHOLE platform, but one person legitimately belongs to two tenants (a subcontractor's PM appears in the main contractor's company and in their own). **The blocking behaviour is HELD, not shipped** — say so plainly, because an earlier cut of this slice did ship it. That cut added a platform-wide `findByEmail` pre-check answering 409 "address taken" across tenants; it was removed before merge on two grounds: it decides an unruled product question, and it is a **cross-tenant existence oracle** — any company admin could enumerate which addresses are registered anywhere on the platform. Current behaviour is therefore exactly pre-B-282: the second tenant's invite returns **201**, the dictionary `user` row is written and kept, and `provision()` raises `CredentialEmailTakenError` which the handler catches, logs server-side (users.ts:239-251) and swallows — so the user sits `invited` with **no credential of their own**, and that particular invite cannot be COMPLETED until this is decided (no auth_user in that tenant → no reset link can be issued against it). The same-company duplicate keeps its scoped 409, unchanged. | (ก) `UNIQUE(company_id, email)` via a SACRED migration (drop `auth_user_email_unique`, add the composite) → one auth_user per tenant, the invite completes normally — **recommended, but it needs a login ruling in the same round**: better-auth signs in by email ALONE, so two auth_user rows sharing an address make the login lookup ambiguous and the sign-in path must learn which tenant is meant · (ข) one identity per platform: the second tenant LINKS the existing auth_user instead of provisioning a new one — no migration, but one password then spans both tenants and a tenant admin's reset affects the other tenant's access · (ค) ratify the block and ship the 409 — accepts the cross-tenant existence oracle · (ง) defer — cross-tenant invites keep landing `invited` and uncompletable, as today | (pending) | 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · non-blocking (nothing regresses; the cross-tenant invite is uncompletable, which is the pre-B-282 state) |
| B-286 | P1-BE-01 · /auth/forgot + /auth/reset — the handlers ship response codes the contract does not declare (SACRED openapi, not touched in-slice) | `openapi.yaml:111-125` (`authForgot`) and `:127-141` (`authReset`) each declare a single `"200": EntityOk`. The merged handlers also answer: reset → **400 INVALID_TOKEN** (auth.ts:394), **400 VALIDATION** (auth.ts:404), **429 RATE_LIMITED** (auth.ts:388); forgot → **429 RATE_LIMITED** (auth.ts:319). The 400 on a dead/expired/replayed token is the endpoint's **primary** failure — the code is right and the contract is short, not the other way round. openapi.yaml is SACRED so nothing was changed here; this row exists so it can be fixed in a contract round. **Impact:** a client generated from the contract has no typed case for the primary failure of the reset endpoint, so the web/mobile reset screens must hand-roll the 400 branch against an untyped response. **G2 does not catch it:** `tests/contract/drift.spec.ts` compares `METHOD path` keys only (drift.spec.ts:93-108) and never inspects declared response codes, so it passes with the gap open. **Precedent:** POST /auth/login already ships an undeclared 429 (auth.ts:250,260) against a 200+401 declaration on dev — pre-existing pattern, not a novel violation. | (ก) declare 400 + 429 on `authForgot`/`authReset` (and login's 429) in a SACRED openapi round, then regen the FE/mobile clients — recommended · (ข) declare only the two 400s, and declare 429 once globally as a shared response since throttling is cross-cutting · (ค) accept the divergence and record it in api-contract.md instead — cheapest, but the generated clients stay blind to it | (pending) | 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · non-blocking (handler behaviour is correct; this is a contract-declaration gap only) |

| B-288 | mobile pm-close — signature capture is UNBUILT, so the screen ships READ-ONLY and its close CTA is permanently disabled | **The close cannot be performed honestly, so it is not performed at all — this is the ruling this slice needs.** Chain of ground truth: (1) `POST /pm/workorders/:id/close` writes ONLY the close fields the body carries — cause/fix/advice and `signature` → `customer_sign` (`apps/api/src/routes/pm.ts` **L785-793**); (2) cause/fix/advice belong to **pm-notes** (step 2) and were already saved there through this same endpoint (B-281); pm-close has no form for them; (3) the only field left is the signature, and it **cannot be captured**: `pubspec.yaml` carries no signature package, adding a native dependency is a Wei-level stack decision, and the TEXT column's ENCODING is undefined — nothing in the repo reads `customer_sign`'s content, only whether it is empty (`apps/web/src/screens/pm/wo-rows.ts` **L206**, `pm_jobs_agg`), so inventing an encoding for a column two platforms share is a contract decision, not a screen decision; (4) therefore the body would be **empty**, and the handler then writes nothing at all (`Object.keys(set).length > 0` is false, pm.ts **L796**), fires the no-op `lineNotifyStub` (B-108b) and returns 200 with the row byte-identical. A 200 that changes no data is not a close, so firing it and reporting success would be a fabricated outcome — and queueing it through the offline sync queue would manufacture a durable op that changes nothing. **The merged WEB port reached the same conclusion independently** (`wo-detail.tsx` DEFAULT 5 / CLOSE SIGNATURE FLAG: “the decorative pad captures no signature, so close sends only cause/fix/advice — `customer_sign` is NEVER fabricated … a UI close does not flip the WO to done”). **What ships:** a READ-ONLY summary — asset name+code (joined `GET /pm/assets`), the checklist tally from the real `items` jsonb, and the signature box rendering the STORED `customer_sign` state, inert (the pm-checklist photo-tile precedent). The sticky CTA is rendered but has **no tap handler at all** (pinned by `pm_close_screen_test.dart`), labelled `pm.closeWithSignBtn` — it names an unavailable capability and never says a job IS closed. **DEVIATIONS from `tests/visual/reference/mobile/pm-close.png`** (enumerated, not hidden): (a) the whole success view (**L185-194**) is dropped — it announces `ปิดงาน PM สำเร็จ` (no status column exists) over `ส่งใบรับรองผล PM ให้ลูกค้าผ่าน LINE แล้ว` (no certificate exists; LINE is a no-op stub); (b) the CTA label `ปิดงาน PM + ส่งรายงาน` (**L213**) is replaced (the `ส่งรายงาน` half is that same LINE promise) — same reasoning as B-285; (c) the pad shows an inert state icon, not the cursive `คุณวีรชัย ✓` (**L207**), and its `แตะเพื่อให้ลูกค้าลงนาม` hint is dropped as a false affordance; (d) the start-end, total-time and parts rows (**L201**) print em-dashes — no clock column exists on `pm_workorder` at all (`checkin_gps` is text coordinates) and no timestamp rides `workOrderWire`, while parts are MONEY living on `pmQuotes.parts` (money = SERVER); the merged web port em-dashes the identical rows; (e) the caption's customer name (**L209**) em-dashes — it is three hops away and `contractWire` stops at `customer_id` (pm.ts L276-288). | **(ก)** keep as shipped: read-only summary + disabled affordance, and open a follow-up slice for signature capture once the stack choice is made — recommended. **(ข)** approve a signature-capture approach so close can be wired: **(ข1)** add a signature package (new native dependency), or **(ข2)** hand-roll pure-Flutter stroke capture (no package needed) — either way Wei must also fix the **storage encoding** for `customer_sign` (stroke JSON? SVG path? data-URI PNG?) so web and mobile agree, since today nothing reads its content. **(ค)** let pm-close POST an empty-bodied close anyway (fires the notify stub, writes nothing) — **rejected in-slice as a fabricated outcome**, listed only for completeness. **(ง)** rule that design fidelity wins on the deviations above and restore the success view / CTA label — note this would make the screen state things no column backs. | | รอ Wei ตอบ — ไม่ block งาน (branch ships; only the close ACTION is withheld) |
| B-289 | B-262 mobile durable offline queue — the durable store has **no `QueryExecutor` on the only build this repo can produce (web)**; vendoring `sqlite3.wasm` + `drift_worker.js` is a stack decision | The rest of B-262 shipped: `AppServices.bootstrap` now opens the drift/SQLite queue (`openDurableSyncQueue`), one shared `QueueDrainProcessor` lives in `AppServices` instead of three screens' `build` methods, and `SyncResumeDrain` supplies the on-mount/on-resume trigger the level-(a) policy doc always promised. **What is NOT shipped is durability on web.** Opening a drift DB needs a `QueryExecutor`. On dart:io platforms that is `NativeDatabase` over a file in the app-documents dir — real SQLite through the already-declared `sqlite3_flutter_libs` + `path_provider`, no new package (`lib/offline/sync_queue_store_io.dart`, proven durable by `test/offline/sync_queue_store_test.dart` against a real on-disk file, close + reopen). **But `apps/mobile` has no `ios/ android/ macos/ linux/ windows/` directory** — the only thing that builds today is **web** (`flutter build web` green, verified before and after this slice). Drift on web needs `WasmDatabase`, which loads two binary assets that must be served from `web/`: **`sqlite3.wasm`** and **`drift_worker.js`**. Neither is in this repo; both are release downloads. Adding vendored binaries (or the platform directories) is a stack decision of the geolocator/B-260 kind, so it is escalated, not decided in the loop. Until then `sync_queue_store_web.dart` returns null and `bootstrap` degrades to `InMemorySyncQueue` — **honestly non-durable on web** (a write queued there still replays within the session, but a browser reload loses it); no fake "saved" is ever shown. | (ก) run `flutter create --platforms=ios,android .` to add the native platform directories — the io executor then becomes the app default with zero further code, and web keeps the honest in-memory fallback (**recommended**: native is the real deployment surface per PLAN.md ภาคผนวก A, and the durable path is already written + tested) · (ข) vendor `sqlite3.wasm` + `drift_worker.js` into `apps/mobile/web/` and add a `WasmDatabase` branch to `sync_queue_store_web.dart` — makes web durable too, but puts two binary blobs in the repo · (ค) both · (ง) accept the in-memory fallback on web indefinitely (queued writes die on reload) | (pending) | 🟡 OPEN (รอ Wei ตอบ — ไม่ block งาน · the rest of B-262 is merged and green) |
| B-290 | web subcon.accept + subcon.progress + subcon.handover — per-element guards on the `cumMap` cumulative markers, the derived contract method, and every period ordinal (DONE · non-blocking · no G5 re-baseline needed) | **Fixed — this is the web half of B-284 option (ค), applied to the two readers that B-277 (i)/(ii)/(iii) and B-284 both named as still trusting the unvalidated `work_period` columns.** The class: *a claim about ONE period was gated by the FIRST period, by a Σ, or by nothing at all.* Sites found and fixed together: **(1) `subcon-accept-rows.ts cumMap`** had NO precondition whatsoever — it summed whatever `pct` arrived in whatever order `seq` imposed, admitted only by the render gate `method === "percent"` where `deriveMethod()` returns the FIRST period's basis. Now returns `null` (view draws no markers and em-dashes `subcon.progressLegend`, which literally describes those lines) unless: every period is percent-basis · every period's own `pct > 0` · `seq` is a usable ordinal (`hasOrdinalSeq`) · Σpct ≤ 100 (the one Σ-shaped check left — it gates a Σ-shaped fact and disqualifies the whole series uniformly, and stops a marker being positioned at `left:140%` and silently clipped by the bar's `overflow:hidden`). **(2) `deriveMethod`** returned the first period's basis and was read as a CONTRACT fact by four renders (title method chip · percent tracker gate · distance/unit tracker gate · handover certificate method row). `work_period.basis` is a per-ROW column with no contract-level counterpart anywhere in the schema or on the wire, so a mixed plan is contract-legal and used to light the percent tracker over metres. Now returns "" unless every period agrees; callers withhold. **(3) the acceptance table's measure-column HEADER** (subcon-accept.tsx) was picked from `method` while each cell picks from its own `p.basis` — the header-vs-row mismatch; a mixed plan was headed "Milestone". Unknown/mixed now em-dashes the header. **(4) every period ORDINAL render**: subcon-progress.tsx L652 (`p.seq === 0` → ดาวน์ stamped on every row of a defaulted all-zero plan), subcon-accept.tsx period column + acceptance-modal title + the AcceptForm accept/reject toasts (prop `periodSeq: number` → `periodLabel: string`, resolved by the parent — the only place that can see the whole plan), and subcon-handover.tsx certificate rows (checked over the WHOLE plan, not the `accepted` subset, because the printed number claims a position in the contract's plan). All gated on `hasOrdinalSeq`; array position is never substituted, that would fabricate an ordinal. `hasOrdinalSeq` is now ONE implementation shared with wo.list (`po-wo-rows.ts`, param widened to the structural `{ seq }`) rather than a second copy that can drift — both modules narrow the same `work_period` rows. **Display SEMANTICS deliberately untouched**: B-277 Ruling 2 (cumulative share vs own share) is still open and is NOT decided here — cumMap's cumulative reading is byte-identical for any plan that passes the guards. This slice only stops a wrong number being printed; it does not choose which right number to print. **G5**: NO re-baseline needed, correcting the parenthetical in B-284 option (ค) ("moves two G5 baselines") — and consistent with B-284's own option (ง). Every guard PASSES on seed: `packages/db/src/seed/index.ts` writes `seq: si + 1` (distinct, 1-based) and `basis: c.basis` uniformly per contract, and the one percent contract (WO-2026-0042) is pct 20/30/25/25 = 100, so markers stay 20/50/75/100, the legend renders, the method chip renders, the "%"/colQty/colMilestone headers are unchanged, and every ordinal prints its real seq. Pinned by a unit test on the exact seed plan plus screen-seam tests; nothing under `tests/visual/` was touched. The live gate was NOT executed in this slice (no running stack). **money = NONE** — nothing here originates, computes or posts an amount; every figure shown is a server column. | (ก) accept as-is: guards are defence-in-depth on the web side, B-284 still owns the data-contract fix at source · (ข) revert the header em-dash only (item 3) and keep the mixed-plan header as "Milestone" for prototype fidelity · (ค) also withhold the whole percent tracker CARD (not just its markers) when cumMap returns null — costs a prototype element but asserts nothing at all | — | รอ Wei — ไม่ block งาน (ทุก gate เขียว · seed ไม่ทริกเกอร์ guard ใดเลย) |

(b) ค่าใน seed คือ mock 7 แถวที่ลอกจาก prototype ใบเดียวกัน โดย 2 คำสั้นกว่าป้ายกราฟของตัวเอง (อ่านเป็นคำที่ถูกตัดหาง); (c) `service_ticket.category` เป็น **free text** ไม่มี enum ไม่มี endpoint list — handler เก็บค่าที่ client ส่งมาตรง ๆ (`apps/api/src/routes/sales-service.ts` L246) — และ **ฟอร์ม create ของ web ที่ merge แล้วเป็น `<input>` พิมพ์อิสระ** (sales-service.tsx L830) ส่วน register render ค่าดิบ (L451 / L708) ⇒ **ไม่มีที่ไหนในระบบประกาศ vocabulary ไว้เลย**. **ที่ ship อยู่ตอนนี้ (แก้แล้วรอบนี้):** srv-new ยังวาด tile ครบ 6 ช่องตาม prototype (§0 กฎ 1) แต่เป็น **honest-disabled** (โทนป้ายจาง กดไม่ได้ ไม่มี GestureDetector) และ **create ไม่ส่งคีย์ `category` เลย** ⇒ คอลัมน์เป็น NULL แทนที่จะเป็นคำที่เดาเอา. พอ Wei ตัดสิน เปิดกลับแก้จุดเดียว: ส่งค่าที่ตัดสินแล้วเข้า `newTicketBody(category:)` ซึ่งรับพารามิเตอร์รออยู่แล้ว. **(2) ป้าย status ซ้อนกันเองใน prototype** — pill ของ `scheduled` ที่ tech-jobs เขียน `นัดแล้ว` (L238) แต่ timeline ที่ srv-track เรียกสถานะเดียวกันว่า `นัดช่าง` (L142); | B-299 | P1-MOB · mobile `exec` — the hero + all four KPI tiles have NO feed; the S-curve legend needs a BAC the /evm response omits; the per-row approve button dropped | Four separate honesty gaps on one screen, all shipped as em-dashes/drops rather than guesses. **(1) The hero (L657-661)** — cumulative sales, units-sold ratio and YoY delta: no endpoint serves any of them. `/sales/*` are per-row LIST endpoints (`land-sales.ts` L925-930), so producing `284.5 M` would mean SUMMING booking rows client-side = originating money. **(2) The KPI tiles (L666-669)** — `กระแสเงินสด 7 วัน` IS served by `GET /dashboard/cashflow-forecast` (`dashboard.ts` L865) but that endpoint is outside this slice's three feeds, so it is a WIRING gap not a backend one; `Project ใกล้กำหนด` and `ขายเดือนนี้` have no endpoint at all; `เอกสารรออนุมัติ` is em-dashed for a subtler reason — the only pending count available (`/dashboard/approvals-inbox`) is TIER-FILTERED to what the caller may act on, so it means "awaiting MY approval", not "awaiting approval". The prototype itself treats them as different quantities (tile 17 vs section 3), so showing the caller's subset under the unscoped label would understate the backlog. The REAL count is shown where it belongs — on the section that says `รออนุมัติของฉัน`. **(3) The S-curve legend (L690-691)** `แผน 65%` / `จริง 62%`: percent-complete in EVM is EV/BAC, and `bac` IS a real stored column (`packages/db/src/schema/finance.ts` L922, "the project total") that `loadEvmSeries` already loads (`evm-series.ts` L49) — but `GET /boq/reports/evm` does not put it in the response body (`series`/`spi`/`cpi`/`currency_code` only). One added field would make both percentages real. **(4) The per-row APPROVE button (L708)** is dropped: this is a read-only screen with no approval-tier logic (that lives on the merged approve/reject sheets), so a button labelled `อนุมัติ` would promise an action it cannot perform. **(5) THE S-CURVE RENDERS ITS EMPTY STATE ON THE SEEDED STACK — proven LIVE, not inferred.** `/dashboard/summary` with no `?project_id` resolves the caller's PRIMARY project as first-by-(`created_at ASC, id ASC`) (`dashboard.ts` resolvePrimaryProject). The seed inserts all 7 projects in ONE statement and `project.created_at` is `defaultNow()` (Postgres `now()` = the transaction timestamp), so all 7 share one identical `created_at` and the tiebreak is `id ASC` → `13ed4a49-…` = **BBT** (`juneflow บางบัวทอง`). `evm_snapshot` is seeded **only** for `project:rjp` (12 rows, `seed/index.ts`). Verified against a freshly migrated + seeded stack this round: grouping `evm_snapshot` by project returns RJP=12 and all six others=0; `GET /dashboard/summary` → `{"project_id":"13ed4a49-…","project_name":"juneflow บางบัวทอง"}`; and the screen's REAL request `GET /boq/reports/evm?project_id=13ed4a49-…` → `{"series":[],"spi":null,"cpi":null,"currency_code":"THB"}`. So the one block on this screen that is fully wired is EMPTY for every seeded caller. **Dropping the project scope does not fix it** — an unscoped read merges every project's snapshots into one list (`evm-series.ts` loadEvmSeries) and would interleave two projects' PV/EV under one period key, drawing a curve that belongs to no project; it only looks correct on this seed by the accident that exactly one project has EVM data. WHAT SHIPS: the scope is kept (it is the same project the rest of the dashboard describes), the card now NAMES that project from `summary.project_name`, and the empty state is an em-dash beneath that name — a true statement about BBT rather than an unattributed blank implying the business has no progress. WHAT IS ESCALATED: which project an EXECUTIVE dashboard should chart at all. Also filed here: the hero gradient's dark stop `#133E6B` (L656) has NO design token, so `brandHover` is used — same direction, lighter stop, a visible deviation from `tests/visual/reference/mobile/exec.png`. | **(1)+(2):** (ก) leave em-dashed until a sales/deadline feed exists [proposed — shipped] · (ข) wire `GET /dashboard/cashflow-forecast` into tile 1 in a follow-up slice (smallest real win) · (ค) build a sales-summary endpoint (cumulative + month + units + YoY) and a due-soon project count. **(2) tile 2 specifically:** (ก) keep em-dashed [proposed — shipped] · (ข) show the caller's tier-filtered count under the unscoped label · (ค) add a tenant-wide pending-doc count endpoint. **(3):** (ก) add `bac` (and/or a server-computed `pct_complete`) to the /evm response body, then wire both legend percentages [proposed] · (ข) leave em-dashed. **(4):** (ก) keep dropped [proposed — shipped] · (ข) make each row NAVIGATE to the existing approve sheet · (ค) build an inline approve with the tier logic. **(5):** (ก) keep the primary-project scope, named on the card, and let the empty state stand — it is honest, and the curve draws the day BBT has snapshots [proposed — shipped] · (ข) give the exec dashboard a PROJECT PICKER so the user chooses the curve's subject instead of inheriting the API's default (adds a control the prototype does not have) · (ค) add a portfolio-level EVM to the server (a real cross-project aggregate — it must be computed SERVER-side; summing project series in the client would be originating money) and chart that on an executive screen · (ง) seed `evm_snapshot` for more than one project so the default dashboard has a curve (makes the demo look right without changing what the screen claims — does NOT address the product question). **Gradient:** (ก) keep `brandHover` [proposed — shipped] · (ข) add a token for `#133E6B` and regenerate. |  | 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · NON-BLOCKING (the shipped screen claims nothing false: every gap renders an em-dash; the approvals card is fully wired down to the SERVER's envelope `total` and the SERVER's `currency_code`; the S-curve is fully wired but EMPTY for the seeded primary project — see gap (5), which is a data/product question, not a broken read. `exec_agg.dart` `ExecKpis` records the per-slot reason in typed form, `exec_screen_test.dart` asserts no mock literal reaches the glass, and `exec_scurve_painter_test.dart` pins the drawn geometry to the served series). |

สถานะจริงของฟอร์มขอลิงก์รีเซ็ต** (1) **429 throttled** — handler throttle ก่อน lookup ทุกครั้ง (auth.ts:111-112,315-322 · 5 ครั้ง/อีเมล+IP · 30 ครั้ง/IP · หน้าต่าง 60 วิ) แล้วตอบ 429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `retry-after: 60` → ข้อความที่ซื่อสัตย์คือ "ขอบ่อยเกินไป · รออีกสักครู่แล้วลองใหม่" ซึ่ง**ไม่มี key**  (2) **transport/server failure** ของฟอร์มนี้ — ก็ไม่มี key เฉพาะ  · ค้นทั้ง dict แล้วพบ generic ตัวเดียวคือ `admin.common.actionFailedToast` = 'ทำรายการไม่สำเร็จ · ลองใหม่อีกครั้ง' (ใช้อยู่แล้ว 11 จอ: admin.subs / admin-pkg-builder / sub.plans / sub.mine / solar.* / om-ticket-view) **ที่ ship ไปแล้ว = ใช้ key เดิมตัวนี้กับทั้ง 429 และ failure (nearest existing key · ไม่แปลใหม่แม้แต่คำเดียว ตาม §0 กฎ 2)** — ข้อความเดียวกันทั้งสองกรณียังปลอดภัยกว่าในแง่ security ด้วย: ไม่มีอะไรที่ผู้ใช้เห็นแปรผันตามว่าอีเมลนั้นมีบัญชีจริงหรือไม่ · **หมายเหตุ 1:** `login.forgotEmailFallback` ('อีเมลของคุณ') กลายเป็น key ที่ไม่ถูกอ้างอิงแล้ว — ปุ่มส่งถูก disable เมื่อช่องอีเมลว่าง แทนที่จะยิงคำขอแล้วบอกว่า "ส่งลิงก์ไปที่ อีเมลของคุณ แล้ว" (path เดียวที่ copy เดิมโกหกชัดๆ) · **หมายเหตุ 2 (ไม่ใช่คำถามในใบนี้):** `login.forgotSent` = 'ส่งลิงก์ตั้งรหัสใหม่ไปที่ {email} แล้ว' ยืนยันว่า**ส่งเมลแล้ว** แต่ delivery ยังเป็น no-op seam — นั่นคือช่องว่างฝั่ง **backend (B-282)** ไม่ใช่เรื่องที่ฟอร์มจะไปแก้ด้วยการเปลี่ยนคำ ฟอร์มจึงคง copy เดิมไว้ตรงตัว | (ก) **คงใช้ `admin.common.actionFailedToast` กับทั้ง 429 และ failure** — ไม่เพิ่ม key ใหม่ (ค่าที่ ship อยู่ · เสนอ) · (ข) อนุมัติ sacred i18n round เพิ่ม key ใหม่ 1 ตัวสำหรับ 429 (เช่น `login.forgotRateLimited`) ให้บอกผู้ใช้ได้ว่า "รอสักครู่" — Wei เป็นผู้ให้ข้อความไทย/en/zh/ar เอง (agent ห้ามแปล) แล้ว web สลับ 2 ข้อความตาม outcome (`throttled` แยกจาก `failed` ในโค้ดอยู่แล้ว — เปลี่ยนบรรทัดเดียว) · (ค) Wei | B-295 | mobile field-pr (feature/mobile-field-group) — the quick-PR write is ONLINE ONLY because an offline replay would create a DUPLICATE purchase requisition | `POST /pr` takes a **client-supplied `no`** (apps/api/src/routes/pr.ts L364) and guards duplicates with a **read-then-insert check** (L399-405: it selects the tenant's PRs and compares `no` in JS). There is no unique index behind it — `packages/db/src/schema/boq.ts` declares `prs.no` as plain `text().notNull()`. So the guard is a race, not a constraint, and the failure mode it leaves open is exactly the one the offline queue would trigger: a create that reaches the server, whose RESPONSE is lost, replays through the SyncProcessor and **creates a second PR** for the same site request. No client-side dedup closes it — the second request is indistinguishable from a legitimate one at the point where it matters. **Shipped behaviour:** this screen is NOT enrolled in the offline queue (the one place it departs from the merged pm-checkin / pm-checklist / pm-notes precedent); it submits online and surfaces a real failure. It also surfaces a THIRD outcome the two-step flow makes possible — `POST /pr` succeeded and `POST /pr/{id}/submit` did not — as a distinct 'saved as draft' state (`pr.form.draftSavedToast`) whose retry re-submits the EXISTING id and never re-creates, because reporting that as a plain failure is precisely what would make a requester raise the same PR twice. | B-297 | mobile FOREMAN/FIELD group — the wire gaps and the two deliberate deviations from the prototype (one ruling asked per item) | Enumerated so none is decided quietly. **(1) fm-accept — the REJECT ACTION is WITHHELD, and goods-receipt rows are READ-ONLY.** **(1a) The one-tap reject is not shipped.** `rejected` is a TERMINAL work-period state: every write of `work_period.status` in subcon.ts is at L666 (`pending`, on create), L784 (`delivered`), L845 (`passed`), L891 (`rejected`), L1020/L1037 (`paid`) — there is NO transition out of `rejected`, and every door refuses it (deliver needs `pending` L754-759 · inspect needs `delivered` / `inspecting` L819-826 · approve-payment needs `passed` L951-957). `POST /defects/{id}/recheck` (L1091-1120) closes a DEFECT row and never touches the period. Meanwhile this screen has NO defect form, so the only reject it could send carries an EMPTY `defects[]`, and the handler's own loop drops every entry with no `item` (L864-871) — zero defect rows written. One unconfirmed tap would therefore permanently fail a subcontractor's work period, with no record of why and nothing able to undo it. The prototype offers no way out either: its reject button (mobile-field.jsx L177) only toasts `ตีกลับ + บันทึก Defect (จากมือถือ)` — it CLAIMS a Defect List was recorded while capturing nothing, the mock mechanic §0 rule 3 forbids reproducing, and inventing a defect form the prototype does not have is forbidden by §0 rule 1. Shipped behaviour: the button keeps its place in the prototype's two-button row as an honest-DISABLED affordance with no tap handler (the merged pm-close B-288 sticky-bar precedent), and the reject path is removed from the code — `kInspectPassPayload` is the only body the slice can build and the repository exposes only `inspectPass()`, so no future caller can open the door by accident. The merged WEB port DOES send a real Defect List (apps/web/src/screens/subcon/accept-form.tsx) because it has an inspection checklist to derive one from; this screen has none. **(1b) goods-receipt rows are read-only.** The prototype filters the acceptance centre to `subcon` + `gr` and renders pass/reject on BOTH (mobile-field.jsx L148/L176-177), but its buttons only toast. `POST /periods/{id}/inspect` takes a WORK-PERIOD id; a receipt's decisions are different doors with different meanings (`POST /gr/{id}/return` / `/cancel`, gr.ts L646/L692). The rows still show (they are genuinely in the queue) with no actions. An already-`rejected` period is withheld for the same reason — the endpoint's own C3 guard would 409 (subcon.ts L819-826). **(2) fm-accept — the money slot is an em-dash.** The wire carries `amount` + `currency_code`, but for 3 of the 4 bases that column is NOT the payable (subcon.ts computeGross: percent → pct/100 × contract.value; distance / unit → per_period_qty × rate_per_unit; only milestone reads the stored amount), `contract.value` is not on this wire, and the gross is server-computed inside `approve-payment` — a different action this screen must not imply it performs. `รอ {n} วัน` (L166), the bold description line (L168) and the `📎` count (L170) are em-dashed too: no wire column. **(3) fm-accept — a 409 and a network failure look the same.** Both render `admin.common.actionFailedToast`, then the queue is re-read so the row's TRUE status shows. A distinct 'another inspector already handled this' message would need a new key. **(4) field-progress — the percentage is withheld.** A work period carries a STATUS, not a completion percentage; its `pct` column is the period's TARGET share under the percent basis, not progress. No ratio is derived from a count of delivered periods either — periods are unequal by construction (that is what the four bases mean), so numerator and denominator would not measure the same quantity. The bar and both delta lines (L328-334) are dropped and the real `status` renders in their place — as its Thai LABEL, never the wire value: `pending` / `delivered` / `inspecting` / `passed` / `rejected` / `paid` are English machine codes and this is a Thai-only field app (§0 rule 2), so the 6 statuses resolve through 4 EXISTING dict keys, ZERO-MINT, using the merged web port's own collapse (subcon-accept-rows.ts mapPeriodStatus + subcon-accept.tsx BADGE_LABEL) so the two clients cannot describe the same period differently: pending→subcon.statusNotReached · delivered + inspecting→subcon.statusRequested · passed + paid→subcon.kpiAccepted · rejected→subcon.rejectBtn. One deliberate departure from the web: an UNKNOWN status renders an em-dash instead of being folded into 'ยังไม่ถึง' — 'not reached yet' is a claim, and a status this build does not know is not evidence for it. **(5) field-progress — the photo grid and the note section are dropped.** The app has no image picker or upload seam AND `GET /subcon-contracts/{id}/periods` returns no acceptance, so no photo can be captured or read; and neither `work_period` nor `acceptance` has a note column anywhere in the schema. Each is an affordance with nothing behind it in either direction. `deliver` therefore always sends `{docs: [], photos: []}` — safe today because the handler only accepts a `pending` period and an acceptance is only ever created BY a deliver/inspect, so there is none to overwrite. **(6) field-progress — a contract PICKER was added.** The prototype pins one contract/period from mock data (L319) and the shell has no route-param mechanism. Rather than ship a screen that can never address a real period, a bare tab route lists the tenant's REAL contracts (`GET /subcon-contracts`) and tapping one loads its periods; a pushed contract id still skips the picker. Nothing is pre-selected. **(7) field-pr — a DOCUMENT-NUMBER field was added, and it has no prototype counterpart.** `POST /pr` requires a client-supplied `no` and there is no issuer endpoint — `GET /doc-numbering` returns the tenant's counters and its own header says issuing-time semantics 'land with the Phase-2 numbering service' (doc-numbering.ts L18-19). Generating one client-side would invent a business identifier the tenant's numbering series owns, so the requester enters it; a collision surfaces the server's 409 honestly. **(8) field-pr — `type` is fixed to `material`.** The prototype offers no type control and the screen is 'quick purchase of a BOQ line from site'; the constant is named in one place (`kFieldPrType`) rather than buried in a request body. **(9) field-pr — the approval-chain card is DROPPED, not em-dashed.** mobile-screens.jsx L467 states `ส่ง → หน.จัดซื้อ → ผจก.โครงการ → ผอ.ก่อสร้าง (วงเงิน ≥ 100K)`; the implemented tiers are ผจก.โครงการ > 500,000 and MD > 2,000,000 (pr.ts, B-070). Rendering it would tell the requester something FALSE about their own document — a claim, not a missing value. The urgency chips and the photo/reason box keep their labels over an em-dash (`pr` has no urgency, photo or reason column; mapping 'ด่วน · 2 วัน' onto `need_date` would invent the mapping as well as the date). **(10) All three screens deviate from `tests/visual/reference/mobile/{fm-accept,field-progress,field-pr}.png`** exactly where the items above withhold or drop mock content — flagged for the G5 round, not silently. | (ก) **[recommended]** ratify items 1a-5 and 8-10 as the correct honest reading (they follow the merged pm-notes B-285 precedent: a missing value is em-dashed, a false claim is dropped), and rule separately on **(6)** the field-progress contract picker and **(7)** the field-pr document-number field, which are the only two ADDITIONS · (ข) revert (6) to the merged approve/reject/pm-checkin honest-empty precedent — the screen then renders 'nothing selected' permanently until a push seam exists · (ค) revert (7) and block PR creation from mobile until a numbering endpoint exists (field-pr becomes a read-only BOQ browser) · (ง) Wei rules per item |  | รอ Wei ตอบ — ไม่ block งาน |

Only the complete badge has a key — `gr.list.badgeComplete`. The prototype renders the partial one as the bare English literal `partial`, which the i18n extraction never captured, so there is NO key for it in `docs/extract/i18n-full.json` (verified: no dict/phrase entry whose value is `partial`; the 6 `*artial*` hits are unrelated sentences). §0 rule 2 forbids inventing a key or hardcoding the literal, so **the complete badge ships and the partial badge is omitted**. | B-270 | contract · POST /gr line `ordered_qty` is optional AND defaults to `qty_ok` — a line can claim completeness it cannot prove | `packages/contracts/openapi.yaml:1609-1622` declares the createGr line object with NO `required` list, and the handler defaults the ordered quantity to the received one (`apps/api/src/routes/gr.ts:451` — `toNum(pick(line,"ordered_qty","orderedQty")) ?? qtyOk`). A client that posts a named line WITHOUT `ordered_qty` therefore reaches the GET /gr wire as `ordered_qty == received_qty`, **byte-identical to a genuinely fully-received line** — so gr.list cannot tell "received everything that was ordered" from "nobody ever said what was ordered", and any completeness rendering (the `gr.list.badgeComplete` badge, the 100% bar) would be asserting a fact the payload does not contain. Not fixable client-side: no other field on the GET /gr wire (nor GET /po / GET /wo, which expose only no/status/amount) carries an independent ordered quantity. **Scope of the web-side mitigation already shipped (feature/web-grlist-rewire):** the badge now additionally requires every line to carry `ordered_qty > 0` and the header total to equal the line total (no unmeasured bare-line quantity) — `isFullyMeasured()` in `apps/web/src/screens/gr/gr-rows.ts`. That closes the bare-line route to a false badge; it CANNOT close this defaulted-ordered_qty route. **Round-3 addendum (same contract latitude, second variant):** because no line field is required, a client may also persist an EXPLICIT `ordered_qty: 0` — the `?? qtyOk` default fires only when the field is ABSENT — i.e. a named line carrying received quantity with no order to measure it against. Summing such a receipt then compares a superset (every line's received) with a subset (only the measured lines' ordered) — the same defect as the header-vs-items pairing, one level down. The web side now treats an unmeasured line as unmeasurable everywhere: no ratio, no bar, no fully-received label, no badge (`allLinesMeasured()` / `itemMeasure()` in `gr-rows.ts`). The wire still cannot say whether that 0 means "nothing was ordered" or "nobody recorded it"; option (ข) below (NULL, not 0) is the only one that resolves BOTH variants. Impact today: low (the seed + the create form always send an ordered quantity); it is a latent honesty hole for any future/mobile client. | (ก) make `ordered_qty` REQUIRED on the createGr line object (sacred openapi + a 400 when a named line omits it) — the receipt then always states what it was measured against · (ข) keep it optional but STOP defaulting: persist `ordered_qty` as NULL when omitted and emit `null` on the wire, so the client can honestly em-dash the ordered side and withhold the badge (needs a migration — `gr_item.ordered_qty` is NOT NULL) · (ค) accept as-is — document that a completeness badge is advisory when the poster omitted the ordered quantity | (pending) | 🟡 OPEN — non-blocking (web mitigation shipped; the residual route needs a contract/BE owner, both files are SACRED) |

Two correctly-configured independent runs: run-2 `gl.inbox 296 px FAIL`; run-4 `petty 936 · gl.inbox 428 · notifications 276` all FAIL (threshold = 160 px = maxDiffPixelRatio 0.0001 of 1.6M). `gl.inbox` fails in BOTH → reproducible, not a fluke. **PROVEN 2026-08-06 from the live payload (was a hypothesis, now evidence):** `GET /api/v1/notifications` returns `created_at: "2026-08-05T18:08:04.742Z"` — the REAL insert instant, not the frozen `2026-07-20T09:00:00Z`. `SEED_FROZEN_NOW` freezes the seed script's `SEED_NOW` and the aging display clock (B-224) but **NOT Postgres `defaultNow()` columns**, so magnitudes GROW with elapsed wall time and these three will worsen daily. All three show the same signature — a 22px-wide vertical text run across table rows (`petty` x1290-1312 · `notifications` x376-397 · `gl-inbox` x1397-1418). **Second, separate defect: `gl-inbox-createdat-b160` is TOO SHORT** — it covers y414-841 but the drift sits at y857-999 (rows added since the rect was measured). **Third, and NOT maskable: `gr-list` is an ORDERING FLAKE** — 0 px on one fresh stack and 253,533 px (15.8%) on the next, on the SAME code, with the whole table body vertically offset. The seed writes every row in ONE transaction so they share a single `now()`; equal sort keys leave row order to the DB. This is a live-G5 flake source that was not previously known, and it is NOT caused by the gr.list re-wire merge (proven: both magnitudes occur without any code change). Superseded hypothesis text follows for the record — the schema uses `.defaultNow()` and the seed sets `created_at` explicitly only for the org tree (`packages/db/src/seed/index.ts:1179`), so every fresh stack seeds a different wall-clock `created_at`. `SEED_FROZEN_NOW` freezes the seed's BUSINESS clock and the ap/ar aging clock — it does not touch the DB's `defaultNow()`. These three screens render a server `created_at` as absolute text; the existing B-160 masks absorb most of it (gl.inbox: 846 masked px differing, 296-428 spilling OUTSIDE the mask), and the spill is consistent with a shorter/longer date REFLOWING the neighbouring column — the exact class already documented for the dashboard `as_of` subtitle.
